L2373: - C2020106_IND (b)(4) Choudhry dated April 7, 2020 (DARRTS Reference ID: 4584158)
L2374: ## Disease Background:
L2375: Atopic dermatitis (AD) is a chronic, relapsing inflammatory skin condition characterized by itching, redness, and scaly rashes, all symptoms that can be painful and cause changes in skin coloring and blisters (American College of Allergy, Asthma &amp; Immunology, 2014).
L2376: ## Investigational Product:
L2377: Abrocitinib (PF-04965842) is a Janus kinase selective 1 inhibitor (tablet), for the treatment of moderate-to-severe AD (b)(4)
L2378: ## 5 CLINICAL OUTCOME ASSESSMENT REVIEW
L2379: ## 5.1 Clinical Trial Population
L2380: The target population for Studies B7451012, B7451013 and B7451029 are as follows:
L2381: ## Studies B7451012 and B7451013
L2382: Adolescent (12 - 17 years) and adults (\ge 18 years) with moderate to severe AD4 and documented recent history (within 6 months before the screening visit) of inadequate response to treatment with topical medications for at least 4 weeks, or for whom topical treatments are otherwise medically inadvisable, or who have required systemic therapies for control of their disease.
L2383: 4A clinical diagnosis of chronic AD for at least 1 year prior to Day 1 and has confirmed atopic dermatitis at the screening and baseline visits according to Hanafin and Rajka criteria (Hanifin &amp; Rajka, 1980). Moderate to severe AD is characterized by (1) Affected body surface area (BSA) of \ge 10\% at baseline, (2) Investigator Global Assessment (IGA) score of \ge 3 at baseline, (3) Eczema Area and Severity Index (EASI) score of \ge 16 at baseline, and (4) Pruritus numeric rating scale (PP-NRS) severity score of \ge 4 at baseline
L2384: ## Study B7451029
L2385: Adults (\ge 18 years) with moderate to severe AD8 and documented recent history (within 6 months before the screening visit) of inadequate response to treatment with topical medications for at least 4 weeks, or for whom topical treatments are otherwise medically inadvisable, or who have required systemic therapies for control of their disease.
L2386: A complete list of the inclusion and exclusion criteria is summarized in PRO Dossier and associated study reports.
L2387: ## 5.2 Clinical Trial Design
L2388: The applicant conducted three pivotal phase 3 trials (Studies B7451012, B7451013 and B7451029). The clinical trial design of these studies is described as follows:
L2389: - Studies B7451012 and B7451013 are identical randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel group, multicenter phase 3 studies to evaluate the efficacy and safety of abrocitinib monotherapy in subjects aged 12 years and older, with moderate-to-severe AD. Both studies were monotherapy trials.
- Study B7451029 is a randomized, double-blind, double-dummy, placebo-controlled, parallel group, multicenter phase 3 study investigating the efficacy and safety of abrocitinib and dupilumab in comparison with placebo in adult subjects on background topical therapy, with moderate-to-severe AD. This study was a trial with background topical therapy.
L2390: In Study B7451012, a total of 387 subjects were included, while in Study B7451013, a total of 391 subjects were included. Subjects were randomized 2:2:1 to receive abrocitinib 200 mg QD, abrocitinib 100 mg QD, or placebo. The trials consisted of a screening period (28 days), a 12-week placebo-controlled, double-blind, treatment period and a 4-week follow-up period.
L2391: In Study B7451029, a total of 838 subjects were included. Subjects were randomized 4:4:4:1 to receive abrocitinib 200 mg QD, abrocitinib 100 mg QD, dupilumab 300mg Q2W, or placebo. The trial consisted of a screening period (28 days), a 20-week treatment period and a 4-week follow-up period. The first part of this treatment period consisted of a 16-week randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, double-dummy treatment period with subjects receiving both injectable and oral investigational products. Following Week 16, subjects previously receiving only placebo would receive abrocitinib 100 mg or 200 mg QD as per their randomized allocation. Subjects previously receiving abrocitinib 100 mg or 200 mg QD continued on their respective dose. Subjects previously receiving dupilumab would take oral placebo.
L2392: Refer to the Clinical review and final study reports for more details on the clinical trial design.

L2401: ## **Reviewer's comment(s):**
L2402: - *The single administration of the PP-NRS at the clinic visits may not provide a complete presentation of the symptom as it may not reflect day-to-day variability in this condition. A daily diary or a serial assessment over a defined period of time are options to assess symptom variability.*
- *PSAAD, Pruritus NRS, and NTIS data were captured electronically.*
- *This reviewer notes that only Studies B7451012 and B7451013 used the PSAAD as a key secondary endpoint. While the PSAAD was administered in Study B7451029, it was not formally tested in the statistical hierarchy.*
- *The NTIS was not formally tested in the statistical hierarchy in Study B7451029, and was viewed as an exploratory endpoint.*
- *Other COAs utilized in the clinical trials includes the following:**Dermatology Life Quality Index (DLQI) or Children's DLQI,*
- *Patient-Oriented Eczema Measure (POEM),*
- *SCORing Atopic Dermatitis (SCORAD),*
- *Patient Global Assessment (PtGA),*
- *Night Time Itch Scale Frequency item,*
- *Patient Health Questionnaire 8 items (PHQ-8),*
- *Hospital Anxiety and Depression Scale (HADS),*
- *Short Form 36 item (SF-36),*
- *Functional Assessment of Chronic Illness Therapy-Fatigue (FACIT-F) or Pediatric FACT-F,*
- *Work Productivity and Activity Impairment Questionnaire: Atopic Dermatitis (WPAI:AD), and*
- *EQ-5D-5L or 5Q-5D-Y.*

L2404: ## 5.4 Targeted Clinical Outcome Assessment-Related Labeling Claim(s)
L2405: The applicant has proposed the following specific targeted COA-related labeling claims (in blue text):
L2406: ## Studies B7451012 and B7451013
L2407: (b) (4)
L2408: ## Studies B7451013 and B7451029
L2409: (b) (4)
L2410: ## **Reviewer's comment(s):**
L2411: *The applicant also proposes tables and figures in the label.*
L2412: *The Division is considering abrocitinib 100mg and 200mg once daily (QD) for approval. The data from Studies B7451012, B7451013, and B7451029 demonstrated that abrocitinib 100mg and 200mg had nominally significant improvement in the PRO efficacy endpoints compared with placebo.*
L2413: *This reviewer has the following recommendations for the PRO labeling claims:*
L2414: - *Due to the single administration of the PP-NRS at the clinic visit and the large amount of missing data (e.g., Studies B7451012 and B7451013 had missing PP-NRS data ranging from 20% to 36% for the study treatment arms at Week 12), it is difficult to interpret the data. As such, this reviewer does not recommend including data from the PP-NRS alone in the label despite the acceptability of the instrument. Based on discussion with Clinical and Biostatistics, they share the same concerns and indicate that these issues can lead to unreliable treatment estimates. To provide supportive information on itch, the PSAAD itch item may be explored in conjunction with the PP-NRS as it was administered daily and does not have high missingness.*
- *Regarding the PSAAD, there are no concerns regarding the reliability and the validity of the instrument. However, it is unknown whether the magnitude of the statistically significant treatment effect is clinically meaningful to patients due to limitations of the external anchor used in the anchor-based analyses. See Section 5.4.6 for more details regarding score interpretability of the PSAAD. We do note that the treatment arms appear to experience change in every item compared to the placebo arm; the total score does not seem to be overly influenced by specific items. Further, change from baseline in the PSAAD total score showed separation between the treatment and placebo arm across a range that likely includes a clinically meaningful change threshold for Studies B7451012 and B7451013.*
- *Regarding the NTIS, in general, an 11-point NRS is acceptable to evaluate itch severity at its worst. However, the NTIS was not formally tested in the statistical hierarchy and does not add any additional information regarding itch severity beyond the PP-NRS and PSAAD item 1 (itch assessed daily with a recall of 24 hours)*
L2415: ## 5.5 Clinical Outcome Assessment(s)
L2416: ## 5.5.1 Clinical Outcome Assessment Description(s)
L2417: ## Pruritus and Symptoms Assessment for Atopic Dermatitis (PSAAD)
L2418: The PSAAD is an 11-item PRO instrument designed to assess the severity of AD signs and symptoms. Each item is rated on an 11-point NRS, ranging from 0 (“Not/No [symptom or sign]”) to 10 (“Extremely [sign or symptom]”). The recall period is the previous 24 hours. See Appendix B for a copy of the instrument (paper version).
L2419: ## **Reviewer's comment(s):**
L2420: *While not the subject of this review, the applicant is also proposing labeling claims from the following instrument(s):*
L2421: ## **Peak Pruritus Numerical Rating Scale (PP-NRS)**
L2422: *The PP-NRS is a single-item PRO instrument designed to assess itch intensity at its worst. Itch intensity is rated on an 11-point NRS, ranging from 0 (“No itch”) to 10 (“Worst itch imaginable”). The recall period is the previous 24 hours. See Appendix A for a copy of the instrument (paper version).*
L2423: ## **Night Time Itch Scale Severity Item (NTIS Severity)**
L2424: *The NTIS is a single-item PRO instrument designed to assess nighttime itch intensity at its worst. Itch intensity is rated on an 11-point NRS, ranging from 0 (“No itch”) to 10 (“Worst itch imaginable”). The recall period is “during the most recent night’s sleep”. See Appendix C for a copy of the instrument (paper version).*
L2425: ## 5.5.2 Conceptual Framework
L2426: The conceptual frameworks for PSAAD is shown in Figure 1.
L2427: **Figure 1. Conceptual Framework for PSAAD**
L2428: ## 5.5.3 Scoring Algorithm
L2429: ## PSAAD
L2430: The PSAAD generates a total score, which ranges from 0 to 10, with higher scores indicating greater levels of AD symptom severity.
L2431: The total score is calculated by summing the item scores and dividing by the number of items with non-missing data (minimum = 6 items). The total score, therefore, is equivalent to the mean of the item scores. A minimum of 6 completed items is required to calculate a total score; if there are less than 6 completed items the score is set to missing.
L2432: **Reviewer's comment(s):** The applicant applied and pre-specified the "half-rule" method which requires at least 50% of the items on PSAAD to be completed. If a patient completed at least half of the PSAAD items, the half-rule procedure of imputation takes the mean of a patient's observed responses on the scale. The half-rule method imputes a person-specific estimate for each missing item based on the average score across completed items on the same scale from the same person; therefore, this imputation method does not depend on data collected from other subjects, other trials, or even other scales from the same subject.
L2433: The applicant did not provide a rationale for not weighting any of the items. However, it is noted that the approach to this condition is similar to that of psoriasis in that when a total score is used, items were given equal weight (see past drug labels for ixekizumab, brodalumab, risankizumab-rzaa, and guselkumab; note that either a disease-specific instrument or an itch NRS was used in the labels).
L2434: Because even a single daily measurement on the PSAAD total score has demonstrated acceptable test-retest reliability, the applicant decided that weekly average PSAAD scores were based on all available data for the 7-day week regardless of how many days the PSAAD score was completed.
L2435: ## 5.5.4 Content Validity
L2436: ## PSAAD
L2437: The applicant completed the following instrument development activities to evaluate the content validity of the PSAAD:
L2438: - Literature review,
- Expert input (consensus meeting), and
- Patient input (concept elicitation and cognitive interviews).
L2439: A summary of the findings from the qualitative research is shown below. For more details on the methodology and results of these activities, see the PRO evidence Dossier.
L2440: ## Literature Review:
L2441: - A total of 17 articles were reviewed, which included 17 instruments.
- Out of the 17 instruments, there were no instruments identified that comprehensively assessed all relevant AD symptoms or impacts. Furthermore, no identified instruments provided sufficient face and content validity.
L2442: ## **Expert Input:**
L2443: - Ten core signs/symptoms were identified by the experts after reviewing findings from the literature review:Pruritus (itching)
- Pain
- Dryness of skin (xerosis)
- Flaking of skin
- Cracking of skin
- Redness of skin
- Bleeding of skin
- Weeping/oozing of skin
- Tightness of skin
- Swelling/inflammation of skin (edema)
L2444: These signs/symptoms were included in a draft instrument.
L2445: **Reviewer's comment(s):** *Thickened skin and discoloration were not included in the first draft of the instrument, while two additional concepts were added to the instrument: sleep disturbance and activities of daily living.*
L2446: ## **Patient input:**
L2447: - The total sample for the concept elicitation interviews consisted of 30 participants with AD (15 adult participants \ge18 years and 15 adolescent participants aged 12 to 17 years).A total of 13 distinct symptoms were reported by participants in the 30 interviews. Of these symptoms, itch was the primary symptom of AD that was reported spontaneously by all 30 participants, while skin thickening was the least frequently reported symptom by participants (n=11/15).
- Itch was reported most commonly by participants as the most frequently experienced, most severe and most bothersome symptom of AD.
- After itch, dryness (n=6/28, 21%) and redness (n=5/28, 18%) were the next most frequently reported symptoms.
- After itch, dryness (8/28) and cracking (5/28) were the next most bothersome symptoms.
- Itching and dry skin were reported to be relevant by all participants (100%).
- Symptoms of redness (87%), bumpy skin (81%), pain (73%), flaky skin (73%), bleeding (67%), discoloration (63%) and swelling (60%) were more relevant to the sample compared to tight skin (43%), cracked skin (40%) and fluid (33%) which was only relevant to a small proportion of participants.
L2448: **Reviewer's comment(s):** *Most AD symptoms were reported by a similar number of adult and combined adolescent participants; however, cracking, fluid, tightness and thickening were all reported by slightly more adult participants than adolescents.*
L2449: - The total sample for the cognitive interviews consisted of 30 participants with AD (15 adult participants \ge18 years and 15 adolescent participants aged 12 to 17 years).
L2450: - Items and instructions of the AD symptom diary were generally well understood (over 93% for each item) and consistently interpreted across all age groups. Of the few participants (less than 5% for each item) who did not appear to understand an item, this was largely due to them never having experienced the symptom themselves or the fact that they talked about other symptoms instead of the symptom assessed by the item currently being discussed.
- Overall participants reported that the 0-10 NRS was easy to use and preferable versus a 0-6 NRS for each item (over 50% for each item except for bumpy [46% for 0-6 NRS vs 38% for 0-11 NRS]; flaking [both were preferred by 43%, while 10% had no preference], and fluid [40% for 0-6 NRS vs 46% for 0-11 NRS]).
- Overall, the recall period ('past 24 hours') of the AD symptom diary appeared to be consistently understood by participants (n=29/30, 97%) across all instructions and symptoms/impact items.
- The final concepts included in the instrument were: itch, pain, dry, flaking, cracked, bumpy, redness, discoloration, bleeding, fluid, and swelling.
- For usability testing (ePRO data collection), a little over half of participants (n=17/30; 57%) completed the diary every day during the 7-day period. The mean scores across each of the 12 symptom severity items were 3.45 or less and participants utilized between 70-100% of the response continuum across the 12 severity items. Zero was the most frequently selected response option across all severity items, while response option 10 was only selected for three of the 12 items: dry skin, bumpy skin and tight skin. The mean scores across each of the 12 symptom bother items were 3.32 or less. Participants utilized between 80% and 100% of the response continuum across the 12 bother items. Zero was the most frequently selected response option across all bother items, while response option 10 was selected for six of the 12 items: itch, dry, bumpy, bleeding, fluid and tightness.
L2451: **Reviewer's comment(s):** Clinical provided the following comments regarding the clinical relevance of the PSAAD items:
L2452: - *There may be certain items that could drive the total score. This reviewer notes, however, that after reviewing the data, the item-level median values at Baseline and Week 12, as well as, the median change values from Baseline to Week 12 do not indicate that there are certain items that are overly influencing the total score. See Section C.7 for item-level statistics.*
L2453: *Overall, this reviewer does not have any critical concerns regarding the content validity of the PSAAD.*
L2454: ## 5.5.5 Other Measurement Properties
L2455: The applicant evaluated the psychometric properties of the PSAAD using data from the phase 2b trial (Study B74510065) and two phase 3 trials (Studies B74510126 and B74510137).
L2456: A summary of the findings is provided for each study. For more details on the methodology and results of these analyses, see the PRO Evidence Dossier.
L2457: ## Study B7451006
L2458: A total of 164 patients in Study B7451006 were included in the analysis population.
L2459: The results are summarized as follows:
L2460: - The applicant performed a confirmatory factor analysis (CFA) to assess the unidimensionality of the PSAAD. The standardized loadings for each PSAAD item was more than 0.4. The Bentler's Comparative Fit Index (CFI), which was used as the primary goodness of fit index, was less than 0.90 (ranging from 0.77 to 0.82). The RMSEA values were &gt;0.05 and the SRMR values were &gt;0.08.
- Item-level ceiling effects (defined as greater than 18% with response 10 in this document [twice the expected probability under a uniform distribution] were demonstrated at baseline.
- For assessment of internal consistency reliability, Cronbach's coefficient alpha ranged from 0.93 to 0.97.
- For assessment of test-retest reliability, the initial intra-class coefficients (ICC) for all available daily PSAAD total scores during the 28-day screening period (Week -4 to Week -1) was 0.81.
- For assessment of convergent validity, moderate to strong correlations (0.67-0.91) were found between the PSAAD and the PRO assessments (PP-NRS, PtGA, PGIS, DLQI total, POEM total) measuring similar concepts related to AD symptoms with the PRO instruments at Week 12, as hypothesized. Additionally, low to moderate correlations (0.24-0.60) were found between the PSAAD and the ClinRO assessments (IGA, EASI, BSA, SCORAD) at Week 12, however, not as hypothesized.
- For assessment of known groups validity, patients in the respective score bands of the DLQI (0-1 = no effect at all on patient's life; 2-5 = small effect on patient's life; 6-10 = moderate effect on patient's life; 11-20 = very large effect on patient's life; 21-30 = extremely large effect on patient's life) at all available time points7 had better PSAAD
L2461: 5 Study B7451006 is a multicenter, randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, 5-arm, parallel-group, dose-ranging phase 2b study to evaluate the safety and efficacy of abrocitinib in subjects with moderate to severe AD. A total of 269 subjects were randomized into the trial.
L2462: 6 Studies B7451012 and B7451013 are identical 16 week randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel group, multicenter phase 3 studies.
L2463: 7 Based on a repeated measures model.
L2464: scores (lower scores) for patient subgroups with lower levels of impact (i.e., “no effect”) than subgroups with greater levels of impact (i.e., “extremely large”).
L2465: - For assessment of responsiveness, the PSAAD showed differences in mean change in the PSAAD total score from baseline to Weeks 2, 4, 8, and 12 between subgroups of patients defined as improved (Much better, Better, A little better) and no change on the Patient Global Impression of Change (PGIC)8 as well as subgroups of patients defined as worsened (A little worse, Worse, Much worse) and no change on the PGIC.
L2466: ## **Reviewer's comment(s):**
L2467: - *Missing data was high (56.7% at baseline, 56.1% at Week 2, 59.1% at Week 4, 63.4% at Week 8, and 65.2% at Week 12), however, because this was a large study there was sufficient sample size to perform some of the analyses.*
- *The fit indices for the CFA indicate a less than adequate fit; however, the results should be cautiously interpreted due to the small sample size (i.e., the ratio of observations per parameter was less than 5, a commonly applied rule of thumb). Due to the lack of interpretability of these results, this reviewer submitted an information request to the applicant to use their phase 3 data to conduct another CFA. See the CFA results for Studies B7451012 and B7451013.*
- *The applicant notes that the ceiling effects observed were most likely a result of the study enrollment criteria.*
- *In general, the results for internal consistency reliability, test-retest reliability, and convergent validity (for PRO assessments) is within acceptable and within reasonable range. The patterns among the correlations between the PSAAD total score and 3 of the 4 clinician-reported measures (IGA, EASI, and BSA) were lower than 0.40. However, the lower correlation is likely due to the fact that clinical examination of visible atopic dermatitis in clinic may not be fully reflective of the patient experience on a day to day basis.*
- *This reviewer does not agree that the defined known groups from the DLQI instrument are appropriate for the assessment of known groups validity as there is limited data to support the numerical cutoffs. Further, the DLQI instrument has intrinsic limitations.*
- *This reviewer notes the following limitations of the PGIC for the assessment of responsiveness:**The PGIC generically asks about a “skin condition” and not specific to atopic dermatitis signs and symptoms.*
- *The PGIC may be susceptible to recall error.*
- *While informative, the PGIC may be a less reliable measure of change from baseline data.*
L2468: 8 Compared to the beginning of the study, how would you describe the severity of your skin condition today? (Much better, Better, A little better; No change, A little worse, Worse, Much worse)

L2478: - A CFA was performed to confirm the unidimensionality of the PSAAD. The standardized loadings for each PSAAD item for each study were more than 0.4. Bentler's CFI was less than 0.90. The RMSEA values were &gt;0.05 and the SRMR values were &gt;0.08.
- For assessment of internal consistency reliability, Cronbach coefficient alphas ranged from 0.95 to 0.97 in Study B7451012; and 0.94 to 0.97 in Study B7451013.
- For assessment of test-retest reliability, ICCs for the weekly PSAAD total scores ranged from 0.86 to 0.95 in Study B7451012; and 0.85 to 0.94 in Study B7451013.
- For assessment of convergent validity, moderate to strong correlations (0.69-0.87) were found between the PSAAD and PRO assessments (PP-NRS, PtGA, DLQI total, POEM total) measuring similar concepts related to AD symptoms with the PRO instruments at Week 12, as hypothesized. Moderate correlations (0.57-0.69) were found between the PSAAD and the ClinRO assessments (IGA, EASI, SCORAD) at Week 12, as hypothesized.
- For assessment of known groups validity, the mean PSAAD scores observed across the Patient Global Assessment (PtGA)9 subgroups showed better PSAAD scores (lower scores) on average for patient subgroups with clearer levels of AD than subgroups with greater levels of severity at baseline and Week 12. Significant differences (P &lt; 0.0001) were found for all comparisons between the PtGA subgroups.
- For assessment of responsiveness, as the PtGA (or IGA) scores improved, PSAAD total scores also improved (negative changes); and when PtGA (or IGA) scores worsened the PSAAD total scores also worsened10. Statistically significant differences (P &lt; 0.05) were found for all pairwise comparisons of the mean PSAAD change scores between the PtGA (or IGA) change scores.
L2479: ## **Reviewer's comment(s):**
L2480: - *In general, the results for internal consistency reliability, test-retest reliability, and convergent validity is within acceptable and within reasonable range.*
- *For the assessment of known groups validity, the applicant formed two PtGA subgroups at baseline (PtGA \le 3 and PtGA = 4) and Week 12 (PtGA \le 2 and PtGA \ge 3) to define their known groups. This reviewer does not agree that the defined known groups from the PtGA should be collapsed as there is no information to support that the collapsed severities are clinically similar. Further, the PtGA instrument may not be an appropriate reference measure for this assessment due to the following limitations.*
L2481: 9 Overall, how would you describe your Atopic Dermatitis right now? ("severe [4]," "moderate [3]," "mild [2]," "almost clear [1]," and "clear [0]")
L2482: 10 Based on a repeated measures model.
L2483: - *The anchor asks patients to assess the severity of their atopic dermatitis with response options of “almost clear” and “clear”; however, some symptoms of the disease (e.g., itch, pain) cannot be appropriately evaluated on clearance.*
L2484: *In general, this reviewer concludes that the evidence generated from the psychometric evaluation of the PSAAD across all three studies supports the reliability and validity of the instrument.*
L2485: ## 5.5.6 Interpretation of Meaningful Within-Patient Score Changes
L2486: The applicant indicated a range of 2.87 to 3 (on a 0 to 10 scale) as a meaningful within-patient change threshold in the PSAAD total score based on a 2-point change in the patient-reported PtGA across Studies B7451012 and B7451013.
L2487: The applicant performed the following analyses to support the proposed threshold range for meaningful within-patient change:
L2488: - Anchor-based analysesDistribution of change on the Skin Pain NRS by change on PGI-S-AD
- Anchor-based empirical cumulative distribution function and probability density function curves
- Distribution-based analyses
L2489: The PtGA was included in phase 3 trials as an anchor to assist in the interpretation of the PSAAD total score. The PtGA is a single-item question asking patients how they rate their AD right now on a 5-point verbal rating scale ranging from “clear” to “severe.”
L2490: A summary of results is provided below:
L2491: - Correlations between the weekly PSAAD total change from baseline scores and PtGA change from baseline were greater than 0.50.
- Patients reporting 2-point change in the PtGA had a mean PSAAD total score change between -2.87 to -3.00.
- Patients reporting 1-point change in the PtGA had a mean PSAAD total score change between -2.00 to -2.04.
- Patients reporting “Better” in the PGIC (administered in Phase 2b study [Study B7451006] only) had mean PSAAD total score change of -2.66.
- The distribution-based estimates were considerably lower than the anchor-based estimates at 1.11 for one-half SD (SD = 2.22) and 0.97 for the standard error of measurement (i.e., 2.22\sqrt{1-0.81}).
L2492: ## Reviewer's comment(s):
L2493: *This reviewer is unable to determine a threshold of clinically meaningful within-patient score change in PSAAD total score due to the following limitation of the PtGA anchor scale:*
L2494: *The anchor asks patients to assess the severity of their atopic dermatitis with response options of “almost clear” and “clear.” The term “clearance” is generally used in relation to the visible signs of atopic dermatitis. In the absence of qualitative data, it is unclear whether the term “clearance” appropriately evaluates some symptoms of the disease (e.g., itch, pain).*
L2495: The PGIC administered in the phase 2b study (Study B7451006) also had limitations:
L2496: - PGIC generically asks about a “skin condition” and not specific to atopic dermatitis signs and symptoms.
- The PGIC may be susceptible to recall error.
- While informative, the PGIC may be a less reliable measure of change from baseline data.
L2497: Based on discussion with Clinical and Biostatistics, they consider a proportion of patients achieving 0 in each item of the PSAAD to be clinically meaningful. The Biostatistics reviewer performed these analyses and noted that there was a small proportion of participants who fulfilled this criterion.
L2498: Figures 2 to 3 presents PSAAD total change scores from baseline to Week 12 by treatment arms for Studies B7451012 and B7451013.
L2499: **Figure 2.** Cumulative distribution function plot of change from baseline in PSAAD total score at Week 12 by treatment group for Study B7451012
L2500: **Figure 3.** Cumulative distribution function plot of change from baseline in PSAAD total score at Week 12 by treatment group for Study B7451013
L2501: **Reviewer's comment(s):** Change from baseline in the PSAAD total score showed separation between the treatment and placebo arm across a range that likely includes a clinically meaningful change threshold for Studies B7451012 and B7451013.

L2541: Diagram illustrating the Mechanism of Action for JAK inhibition on Atopic Dermatitis.
The diagram shows an Autocrine loop involving Keratinocytes and Effector Lymphocytes.
Keratinocyte Pathway:
RNA/DNA activates TLR-dependent and -independent pathways.
These pathways lead to the production of Type I IFNs and Inflammatory Cytokines (IFN\alpha and IFN\gamma).
IFN\alpha binds to IFN\alpha/R (IFNAR) receptors.
IFN\gamma binds to IFN\gamma receptors.
Both IFNAR and IFN\gamma receptors activate JAK1 and TYK2.
JAK1 and TYK2 activate STAT.
STAT leads to the production of IFN-inducible chemokines (CXCL9, CXCL10, CXCL11).
A JAK1 Inhibitor is shown blocking the JAK1/TYK2/STAT pathway.
Effector Lymphocyte Pathway:
Chemokines (e.g., CXCL10) bind to CXCR2 receptors on Effector Lymphocytes.
Effector Lymphocytes produce CXCL3 and CXCL10.
Microscopic images show CXCR3 and CXCL10 expression.

L2544: Flowchart illustrating the Multiple Testing Procedure for Trials B7451012 and B7451013.
The procedure starts with:
Test IGA and EASI75 for 200 mg QD vs placebo at W12 at \alpha = 0.05
Test NRS4 for 200 mg QD vs placebo at W2 at \alpha = 0.025
Decision point: Is Hypothesis Rejected?
If Yes: Test the hierarchical testing procedure with the order specified in Sequence A at \alpha = 0.05.
If No: Test the hierarchical testing procedure with the order specified in Sequence A at \alpha = 0.025.
Sequence A (Hierarchical Testing Procedure):
Test NRS4 for 200 mg QD vs placebo at W4
Test NRS4 for 200 mg QD vs placebo at W12
Test IGA and EASI75 for 100 mg QD vs placebo at W12
Test NRS4 for 100 mg QD vs placebo at W2
Test NRS4 for 100 mg QD vs placebo at W4
Test NRS4 for 100 mg QD vs placebo at W12
Test PSAAD CFB for 200 mg QD vs placebo at W12
Test PSAAD CFB for 100 mg QD vs placebo at W12
NOTE: Solid arrow indicates statistical significance has to be achieved in order to test the subsequent hypothesis.
NRS4 = Improvement of >4 points in the pruritus NRS score for severity;
CFB = Change From Baseline
L2545: Flowchart illustrating the statistical testing procedures for the trial endpoints.
Left Path (Sequential Testing):
Test IGA and EASI75 for 200 mg QD vs placebo at W12 at \alpha = 0.05
Test IGA and EASI75 for 100 mg QD vs placebo at W12 at \alpha = 0.05
Test the hierarchical testing procedure with the order specified in Sequence A at \alpha = 0.025
Test the hierarchical testing procedure with the order specified in Sequence B at \alpha = 0.05
Right Path (Sequence A and Sequence B):
Sequence A:
Test NRS4 for 200 mg QD vs placebo at W2
Test NRS4 for 100 mg QD vs placebo at W2
Test NRS4 for 200 mg QD vs dupilumab at W2
Test NRS4 for 100 mg QD vs dupilumab at W2
Sequence B:
Test IGA for 200 mg QD vs placebo at W16
Test EASI75 for 200 mg QD vs placebo at W16
Test IGA for 100 mg QD vs placebo at W16
Test EASI75 for 100 mg QD vs placebo at W16
NOTE: Solid arrow indicates statistical significance has to be achieved in order to test the subsequent hypothesis. Dotted arrow indicates statistical significance has not been achieved for all hypotheses.
NRS4 = improvement of \ge 4 points in the pruritus NRS score for severity.

L2557: Figure 19 displays four plots assessing the goodness-of-fit for the Final Population PK Model. The plots are arranged in a 2x2 grid, with the bottom row showing conditional weighted residuals and the top row showing observed concentration versus predicted concentration.
The top row consists of two scatter plots comparing Observed Concentration (ng/mL) on the Y-axis against predicted concentration on the X-axis. The left plot uses Individual Predicted (ng/mL) on the X-axis, and the right plot uses Population Predicted (ng/mL) on the X-axis. Both plots show observed concentrations ranging from 0 to 6,000 ng/mL. A diagonal line (y=x) and a fitted curve (likely a power function) are overlaid, indicating a good fit.
The bottom row consists of two scatter plots showing Conditional Weighted Residuals (Y-axis) versus time or predicted concentration (X-axis). The left plot shows residuals versus Time After First Dose (hours), ranging from 0 to 2,000 hours. The right plot shows residuals versus Population Predicted (ng/mL), ranging from 0 to 3,000 ng/mL. Both residual plots show residuals centered around zero, with dashed lines indicating the 95% confidence interval (approximately \pm 2), suggesting the model adequately captures the variability.
A legend below the plots indicates the DOSE groups: 3, 30, 200, 600 (represented by blue, green, orange, and dark blue/purple circles, respectively) and 10, 100, 400, 800 (represented by red, magenta, pink, and light green circles, respectively).

L2570: Diagram illustrating the metabolism and clearance pathways of Abrocitinib and its metabolites.
The Parent Active compound is metabolized via CYP2C19/9 and 3A/2B6 to M1 Active. M1 Active is cleared via:
74% Renal CL (OAT3/passive)
18% Metabolism CL (21% CYP450/79% Other)
8% Feces CL
The Parent Active compound is also metabolized via CYP2C19/9 to M2 Active. M2 Active is cleared via:
94% Renal (OAT3/passive)
2.5% Metabolism CL
3.5% Feces CL
Both M1 Active and M2 Active are metabolized via CYP2C19/9 and 3A to M4 Inactive. M4 Inactive is cleared via 1% Renal (net reabsorption).
L2571: Figure 33 is a box plot illustrating Dose-Normalized AUC (\text{ng}\cdot\text{h}/\text{mL}/\text{mg}) in Single Dose Studies, categorized by Combined CYP2C19 and CYP2C9 phenotypes and Overall Phenotype Category (Wild-type, Reduced, Elevated, Mixed).
The Y-axis represents Dose normalized AUCinf (\text{ng}\cdot\text{h}/\text{mL}/\text{mg}), ranging from 0 to 60. The X-axis shows the Combined CYP2C19 and CYP2C9 phenotype categories: EM/EM, EM/MM, EM/PM, IM/EM, IM/MM, PM/EM, RM/EM, RM/MM, and UM/EM.

EM/EM
EM/MM
EM/PM
IM/EM
IM/MM
PM/EM
RM/EM
RM/MM
UM/EM
The plot shows that the Wild-type (EM/EM) phenotype has the lowest AUC. The Reduced phenotype categories (EM/MM, EM/PM, IM/EM, IM/MM, PM/EM) generally show increased AUC compared to Wild-type. The Elevated phenotype categories (RM/EM, RM/MM) show decreased AUC compared to Wild-type. The Mixed phenotype category (UM/EM) shows a moderate increase in AUC compared to Wild-type.
Overall, the Reduced phenotype category shows the highest AUC values, while the Elevated phenotype category shows the lowest AUC values.
